ParlaMint-AT_2000-09-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00037_d7e2991	The following changes are planned: Firstly, the extension of the threat of punishment to life-long imprisonment against the leadership of drug rings. – Current framework: 10 to 20 years of imprisonment. Secondly, the increase in the minimum penalty to three years for drug dealers working as gang members in the repeat case or as members of a large band or to whom an excessive amount of addiction has to be charged. – Current criminal framework: one to fifteen years. Thirdly, the consideration of new methods of communication in the criminal record of the call for or the approval of drug abuse. Fourthly, the restriction of the possibility of a provisional ad deposit if the perpetrator is again indicated for acquisition or possession of a small amount of addiction for its own use during the open probation period after an ad deposit has already been filed. This draft ministerial draft has already been sent for review. However, it is imaginable that the Parliamentary Committee of Inquiry will once again refer and review these criminal frameworks to each other.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-09-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00037_d7e15416	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! I am grateful that I have been given the opportunity to comment on this issue, on which nothing other than fog reports have been placed in the public over the last few days. Ladies and gentlemen! First: The Austrian forests are in an excellent condition in international comparison. This proves the annual forest inventory, and that says a study by the University of Vienna, which confirms a high quality class of Austrian forests. Ladies and gentlemen! Secondly, The good legal basis for this is the Austrian Forest Act, which for many decades has ensured that the Austrian forests can fulfil their four functions: the utility function, the protection function, the recovery function and the welfare function. This is the Forest Act, which ensures the sustainability of all Austrian forests and, since 1975, ensures the free movement of forests. The property structure to which this Forestry Act applies is also clear. 80 percent of the Austrian forest is privately owned and is carefully maintained and held by private forest owners. About 20 percent are owned by the public sector, of which about 15 percent are owned by the Republic, ladies and gentlemen, and only about 30 000 hectares are owned by the Austrian Federal Forest AG, which means less than 1 percent are owned by the AG. I am proud of the exclusion of the Austrian Federal Forests because it has provided a good basis for sustainable development in the sense of the forest economy and the economy. The Bundesforeste AG delivers higher yields than we used to, and maintains the forest very carefully, ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] On the basis of this good law, I have now submitted a novel of the Federal Forest Act and sent it for consideration which has the content, ladies and gentlemen, that we transfer the standing waters of the public water goods – it could be easier to say the lakes – to the administration of the Austrian Federal Forest AG, as far as they are not yet owned by the Federal Forests. This concerns Lake Wörther, Lake Ossiacher, Lake Millstätter, Lake Weissensee, Lake Brennsee, Lake Afritzer, Lake Längsee, Lake Pressegger, Lake Baßgeigen and Lake Attersee in Upper Austria. [[Abg. Leikam: That's all in Carinthia!]] These lakes remain the property of the Republic, Mr Deputies. [[Abg. Dr. Khol: The Attersee is in Upper Austria!]] They obviously did not read the novelle that came out. Ladies and gentlemen! Due to this transfer, the substance value of the Austrian Federal Forest is increasing, because income from these lakes can ultimately also be achieved. In return, the Austrian Federal Forest AG makes a payment of about 3 billion schilling to the owner, to the Republic of Austria. This is the content of the legal framework I have proposed. The Austrian Federal Forest will, of course, also use the land transport to cover these amounts. Yes, the Austrian Federal Forests have a basic transport strategy, which, ladies and gentlemen, was unanimously adopted in the Supervisory Board. This strategy does not provide for the disposal of core areas, the sale of strategic resources, the sale of peripheral and scattered areas and the consideration of local and local interests. This sales strategy is also the basis for the necessary steps in the sale of land and on the ground, ladies and gentlemen, and that is nothing new. In 1988, for example, the Federal Forests in Gußwerk sold 640 hectares, in Rosenbach-Millstatt about 250 hectares or in Krems 1 185 hectares. Ladies and gentlemen! I ask you to leave the church in the village during this discussion. Those responsible in the Federal Forests estimate that an area of between 30 000 and a maximum of 50 000 hectares may be affected. That is between 1 per cent and 1.5 per cent of the Austrian forest area, ladies and gentlemen! Finally, I would like to address three questions to you. Can you answer the question why, in view of this state of Austrian forests, of which 80 per cent are managed in private property, the private forest owners should suddenly operate better than the Federal Forest, or vice versa: You suspect the risk that the private would not comply with the Forest Act? The private forest owner has demonstrated for many decades that he is concerned about sustainable forestry. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Ladies and gentlemen! Secondly, This land transport also provides farmers with the opportunity to make a contribution to the preservation of their substances. Thirdly, Of course, the Federal Forests must also comply with the legal obligations of the Federal Forest Act and the Novelle, for example in the area of seashore policy, which is a better protection of substances than we had previously in the area of seashore land. The Federal Forests will submit a decision on how to deal with the shores of the sea and how to deal with them. The Federal Government as its owner must carry out the necessary safeguards. Deputies! Let me make one last comment: I can recall exactly the parliamentary debate on the extension of the Austrian Federal Forest AG. I think that the current debate is actually a compliment to the Federal Forests for the good work they do, and is also a very peculiar contradiction to the discussion three years ago, because at that time it was said that the transformation into the AG is breaking the catastrophe over the Austrian forest. The opposite is the case. You can be sure that I will continue to ensure the sustainable management of the forest and the gentle and sustainable conservation of the water resource in the future. Thank you.

I admit that decoupling the cost bill for teaching and research from healthcare is not easy, but it is at least better to do than before. It will therefore be necessary in the ministry to make sure that some kind of equality of arms is established in the negotiations, i.e. for something that devours almost 40 percent of the resources, not just one hundredth of the civil service. A study at the AKH Vienna, which is relatively critical, clearly shows the situation of the federal government. The AKH was compared to 13 acute hospitals. The performance at the AKH – I now only cite two examples, otherwise it takes too long – in the area of operating services at brain vessels were 27 times as high as at all 13 comparable Vienna acute hospitals, in the case of open heart interventions five times as high, and I can extend the list by the factor 10. These services – and they are the most expensive services according to LKF or KRAZAF, as you want them to do – in turn only have to do with research and teaching. That is, the federal government is one of the best financiers of the health care of the countries. I know they have no joy either, I know they have no money either, but fairness and clear and open cards are asked here. The development of the costs of administration at the AKH Vienna is remarkable, and if I tell you this, it is not a polemic against Vienna, but a clear position to strengthen, so to speak, the negotiating rail of the federal government. If one compares the administrative costs of the AKH Wien with Graz and Innsbruck, the costs of the administration of the AKH are 20 times higher than those of Graz, even if one evens it on bed equality, and 30 times higher than those of Innsbruck. Administrative costs are at least related to research and teaching, and I understand very well if the woman minister is no longer prepared to use research resources in the sense of an ordinary woman. Furthermore, the dual union between the Federal Government and the Länder is damaging, i.e. the mixed relationship between the University Hospital and the Landeskrankenhaus, as is most acute in the personnel sector in Innsbruck and Graz. To date, every minister has understood this, but ultimately has not the force to change it. I would also like to remember: Austrian university clinics are among the largest in the world. You can be proud of it. But if you know that in about 300 anaestheists are working as a university teacher and that these could loosely cover the entire teaching and research for the Central European area, I ask you if you do not have to think about it here. Faith But not that the clinics don't need this money! The question is whether the right person pays for it. There is enough maladministration in the clinic sector – and I have once had a very long discussion about it – and a refusal to pay in particular in the field of research on behalf of third parties, which prevents the Republic and the Ministry from holding billions of money. Mrs Gehrer has written an excellent letter to all the board members, and I know, and I can assure you that most of the ordinations did so after recently reading and said: We're not responding to that. – If you let this happen, you violate the responsibility you have as a citizen. Mrs Gehrer has tried, but I would ask you to show even less shyness where there are shortcomings. I would also ask you to support the Court of Auditors. The most beautiful reports are useless if people can get up and say in official meetings of faculty colleges, I don't care, and thus still find applause. I don't attack my colleagues, I just don't defend black sheep. Last word on the subject of secondary employment: I know this is a problem, much bigger in Vienna than in Graz and Innsbruck. I personally do not defend any sheep in any color. In this case, the Ministry should submit that the obligation to report is laid down by law in the direction of an authorisation obligation. That won't give me any friends, but there's a certain degree of propriety. I would ask you, however, not to deal with this matter in 8 minutes, but to debate it more often. Thank you.

The Belvedere Gallery is a good part of Austrian history, and many of the listed works cannot be found at the moment. The question now arises: Why can't these works be found? It is even suspected of a crime in this regard. But: What is really the reason for this? The time and perhaps also the carelessness of several generations of museum staff is the reason. Through perhaps sloppy, outdated inventory lists, which have been led to this in anachronistic way, it has come to the conclusion that many works can no longer be found. But there are also some entries from the past, such as abbreviations or accelerators, no longer to identify, and some puzzles can no longer be solved. It must also be borne in mind that many of the works listed on the lists have been attributed to the former crown countries in the Treaty of Saint-Germain, the peace agreement between Austria-Hungary and the West Powers. So that's far behind. I do not want to talk about the problems of the Second World War, and I do not want to talk about how many works disappeared at that time. Time witnesses can hardly be found for this. For example, many of these works could have been burned in the bombing and shooting of Budapest or could be located in the former crown countries. Ladies and gentlemen! Looking for the faults only in today's administration would be unfair and passed the root of the problem, which is probably much deeper. You might as well say: It is a chronicle of Austrian history. Perhaps it is opportun to charge the mistakes of the past to the responsible people today, at least it is not fair. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! It is also necessary to admit to the responsible person that they do their best to explore the place of storage of these works. The museum has not hesitated to respond to the criticism of the Court of Auditors and has handed over a list of works. When it became clear that the own investigations did not continue, the security authority was switched on. The Ministry has also not hesitated to immediately form the task of setting up a working group of representatives and representatives of the Federal Ministry of Finance and the Federal Ministry of the Interior, and it is also important that experts of the Court of Auditors participate. However, these experts are now facing an almost insoluble task, because most of the witnesses have, unfortunately, already died. A positive example of this is the investigation in connection with the Poiret Foundation. My dear colleagues! We have to accept that for some things from the past, there is simply no one left alone. I think it is important that the Belvedere Gallery has drawn the right conclusions from the Court of Auditors' criticism and has taken appropriate steps.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-09-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00037_d7e20568	Mr President. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I should like to thank you for your support. High house! The Court of Auditors is required by law to take all possible steps to reduce or avoid expenditure, and in this context it has to make proposals aimed at ensuring a more economical conduct both in the federal and local sectors. Of course, the Court of Auditors has not only limited itself to such a point in time, but has always made fundamental structural proposals, not just recently, but for a long time, regardless of various savings packages made by the governments of various compositions in recent years. These structural reform proposals relate, for example, to the pooling of financial country directors, to the rethinking of our judicial organisation and, among other things, to, Mr Leikam, whether the organisation of our executives is optimal or whether it could not be improved. In the field of executives, it seems to the Court of Auditors, given the budgetary burden that this entails for the public sector, to be advised to deal closely with it. Police and Gendarmerie receive a total of around 20 billion schilling from the budget each year. Therefore, if it succeeds in achieving a reduction here through a merger or at least through better cooperation – perhaps only in sub-areas, later in larger areas – it could move a lot, but above all it could be achieved by saving billions. [[In the case of the liberals as well as the aggregator, I should like to thank you for your support. Friend.]] Considering that savings in the order of only 5 per cent – and they can be achieved without a doubt through synergies – already have 1 billion schilling in savings, at 10 per cent even 2 billion schilling, then we should be worth rethinking the organisation of the executive and perhaps making proposals for it. If, in the field of the private sector today, there is and is a reputation for the fact that synergies occur in particular – such as in the purchase of the CA by Bank Austria – if this is promoted in the private sector and is actually approved by all parties that there are such measures, then such a measure cannot be denied as a model for the public sector and by the public sector. I am very much concerned that the discussion is about the fact that, seriously from all sides, including those who may not necessarily befriend themselves with such a consideration, we are now prepared to do more detailed studies, and that we are concerned about where there is a way to make savings. Mr Leikam, I would like to make one point: From my side, in connection with the suggestions regarding police and gendarmerie, the proposal has never been made or even the demand that there should be staff savings. [[Abg. Leikam nodds to that.]] Especially in the field of executives, I don't actually see them. And much less – I would also like to say that with all the clarity – I am a follower of the so-called lawn mower method, so that savings are made at a certain percentage across the entire federal administration or even over a few larger areas and without regard to the tasks to be performed by the officials concerned. [[Detonatative condolences of the Abg. Leikam]] [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] [[Abg. Leikam: ( towards the government bank) Mr. State Secretary! Just watch out!]] There is a need for relinquishing tasks at first – and there are enough relinquishing tasks in the federal administration – then one can only take into account personnel savings. I also believe that, in the context of the major reform, which has now been announced – whether in the federal administration, or in part in the field of the countries and communities – one should seriously ask whether one should leave it only in individual actions or whether one should not actually undertake in-depth reforms. If the Court of Auditors' proposals for savings, they may also be unconventional and they may not be majority-able at the first moment, then it would lead to a closer relationship with them and that, in the end, savings can actually be achieved, the Court of Auditors would be very pleased, and it would be very pleased to do so. [[In the case of the liberals and the ÖVP, as well as the Abg. Hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, ……]] Let me now turn to the real issue of today's negotiations. There are three reports from the Court of Auditors which are under negotiation: a report on income in the field of the national economy, the follow-up to the 1998 activity report and the report on the financing of political parties and clubs. I would particularly like to turn to the latter report, because this report is an extraordinary one; something extraordinary because in the past there was nothing comparable on the part of the Court of Auditors. The Court of Auditors was therefore responsible for examining the political parties and the parliamentary clubs because it was based on a unanimous decision of all the parties represented in the National Council. The Court of Auditors has had a very extensive task. He examined the five political parties represented in the National Council and the five parliamentary clubs at that time, and also the Parliamentary Directorate as the body paying the subsidies to the parliamentary clubs. He also examined the Federal Chancellery, which is responsible for the political parties receiving the corresponding subsidies. He clearly identified shortcomings in the course of his examination; that was to be expected. In this context, the Court of Auditors, in particular, criticised the lack of sparingness of the political parties and the clubs. All in all, however, I would like to say that everything in all of the report on the parliamentary clubs and the political parties has been so out of place that we can come to the conclusion that the political parties and the clubs have a much better way of acting than perhaps nationally and publicly. [[Demonstrativ accompaniment of the Abg. Dr. Niederwieser.]] The conduct of the political parties and also of the parliamentary clubs was by no means influenced by negligence and wastefulness. I would like to say that with all the clarity here, and in this way we have included this in the report. Nevertheless, there were, of course, certain criticisms. As far as Parliament's management is concerned, the Court of Auditors has been able to confirm its full freedom of choice. This is also to be expressed with all clarity. [[Conjugation with the SPÖ and the Abg. May. Trattner.]] The Federal Chancellery has also acted legally and properly in dealing with the award of subsidies. The Court of Auditors, as a result of its examination, appeared to have fewer criticisms towards the political parties and also the parliamentary clubs in the first place, rather it seemed appropriate to indicate some very specific weaknesses in the framework under which subsidies for the political parties are to be made. And two of these weaknesses I would like to take out particularly. For the first time, this is the open question, which is to be understood by "public relations". The political parties are entitled to support for the purposes of public relations under the Party Law. In other words, the political parties have committed themselves to using the funds given to them for public work. However, in order to be able to carry out an examination of whether such a dedication has actually been observed or not, a clear definition of the concept of public relations needs to be defined. And here it seems to the Court of Auditors to be extremely ill. Indeed, the Court of Auditors, in accordance with the opinion expressed in the scientific paper, has understood, by public work, an external information, an advertising activity of the parties. The political parties – essentially in their entirety – have used a much wider concept of public relations in their activities. The political parties have shown us in their opinions that, in their view, the entire activity of the political parties should be subsumed under the concept of public relations, including – and this is particularly significant – the internal effort. And here, in principle, the Court of Auditors disagrees. Although he acknowledges that the legislator at the time has failed to provide a clear, clear definition of the concept of public relations, he believes that this failure of the past should be remedied. If one were to base the term "public work", as the political parties understand it, then this would mean that any examination of the intended issue of subsidies would be obsolete, because then simply any use of these funds would be subject to the term "public work". And that, says the Court of Auditors, cannot be read out of the law. Therefore, the Court of Auditors has included in its report the recommendation that the legislator should clarify the term "public work". Whether it does so by means of an authentic interpretation or in a novel to the Party's law, the Court of Auditors does not seem to be essential, it can choose one as the other way, but in the Court of Auditors' opinion it is called upon to make a clear clarification in so far as this is in the interests of all. A second vulnerability has been identified by the Court of Auditors in connection with the lack of recovery of subsidies granted to political parties. In contrast to other subsidisers, there is no provision in the view of the political parties that in the event of a non-disciplinary use of funds, the funds must also be repaid or recalculated by future means. The Court of Auditors considers that the political parties should also be treated in the same way as all other subsidy beneficiaries in that Republic, and that the legislator should ensure that, in the event of an improper use of funds, a return on the part of a political party may be made if this is found. In this context, I should also like to refer to similar provisions which already apply to subsidies to the educational institutions of the political parties. These provisions, which already apply to some of the political parties, should also be applied analogously to the political parties themselves, as in the case of the parliamentary clubs. I mean, if the legislator – and with this you are all called – made a revision of the party law in this respect, then many critics would have been taken out of the winds in the conduct of the political parties. From your side could be Evidence is given that you are serious with a real control of the funds for the political parties and that you are also subject to the same conditions applied to other subsidy beneficiaries of this Republic. It was very much thanks today to all the groups here in the House to hear the Court of Auditors and the Court of Auditors. I recorded it with great goodwill. On the occasion of the examination of the political parties and the parliamentary clubs, I would like to express my gratitude for the good cooperation of the political parties and the parliamentary clubs. [[General condolences.]] Therefore, allow me to add to this thanks the wish that you should follow the Court of Auditors' recommendations just as this examination has taken place. This would clearly demonstrate the seriousness with which you accept these recommendations from your side.

We will not give our approval to the Cultural Report. ParlaMint-AT_2000-09-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00037_d7e22073	The next speaker was Mrs Wolfmayr. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-09-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00037_d7e22094	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! High house! The present cultural report is an impressive precipitation of the work of officials of the Federal Museums and a proof of their activity and of the reorganisation carried out in 1998. I would like to focus on a number of important points and to develop from them a few ideas or perspectives of current museum work. My previous speaker, Dr Povysil, has already addressed the number of visitors who have a satisfactory increase of 11 percent in 1998 compared with 1997. In particular, the entry-free 26. October, this Austrian museum day, has proved to be a very good advertising measure, and my suggestion to do so would be: I have heard that in England there are museums that have this open day once a week. The Essl Collection in Austria has also apparently included this suggestion. This is a very good advertising tool, which now beats less for tourism, but is very good for families and students and, as has been shown, has a sustainable advertising effect. It turns out that the visit of a museum can be interesting, exciting and also fun – with the new museum didactics, which is partly applied or already understood in the beginning. The times are God's thanks, when museums were still "Church for Collectors", in which whispering visitors marched from exhibition space to exhibition space. I am referring to a presentation by Friedrich Waidacher, the former director of Joanneum Graz. Rather, the museum today sees itself as an institution on behalf of and service of society, in which not objects, but man is at the heart of the human being, as creator, collector, user of the represented, as a person concerned, as a narrator, as a spectator, as an experiencer and as a follower. The museum is understood as a place where selected testimonies of a certain reality are collected, preserved and researched, which are accessible to everyone. This is no longer taking place at a learning location or in a classroom, but – again quote – at a specific place of informal, free and potentially creative encounter with real objects. We know that public relations are of particular importance. As mentioned in the cultural report, this serves a monthly programme flag, annual posters, posters of the school, the cultural report itself, and what is more perceived and intensively used every day, the Internet. It is also noted in the cultural report that visitor kindness needs to be further increased, which is very important. It is about the importance of museum pedagogy in the context of intensive cooperation between cultural institutions in the school sector and the adult education sector. Ladies and gentlemen! I have never particularly liked the word "popular" before, but it is primarily about education and learning. Education for children is of particular importance. The museum didactics especially for children has taken very new and very interesting paths. It is therefore about education for children and adults, the knowledge of their own roots and origin, the knowledge of the past and the culture in which one has grown up. In this context, I would like to point out a project which unfortunately has not yet reached the planning stage, which is the plan of the completely redesigned and redesigned Volkskundemuseum in Graz. The Geramb-Enkel, adult designer and Joanneums curator, Heiner Herzog, wants to realize this museum, which has been revitalised in the framework of the project "Graz – Kulturstadt 2003". I can say that I fully support this idea. Mr. Cap! They have called for the encounter of contemporary creation in connection with museum art. There have been attempts to do this, and I would invite you to look at it. You can retrieve the reports in Joanneum. This encounter of modern artists takes place there. The project has already been implemented in its first stage, but is extended. Contemporary artists react to exhibition objects, and this is then communicated again. According to the Federal Constitution, only the federal museums fall into the competence of the federal government; the affairs of the other museums are state-owned. However, in order to ensure the cultural presence of the federal government also in the countries, local and municipal museums are very much subsidized, which is in line with the federal principle. Unfortunately, however, much too little space is still being devoted to promoting regional structures. I propose that this should be much stronger in the future. A lively exchange between Vienna and the federal states would do us good in the sense of a more intensive networking of federal and state, which is also wanted. For example, successful large exhibitions could now be brought to the federal states or vice versa, because we also have very interesting exhibitions in the federal states. The topic is very complex, very multilayered, very diverse and understood in development. The present cultural report shows a status quo with new possibilities for future museum work and objectives – that is to say, 1998 – at the time. I would like to say, however, that this point is particularly important to me and that in this context it is also possible to see the folklore museums. When I turn attention to the regions and their differences, I thus awaken the awareness of individuality in me and in those with whom I share and share my experiences. I would like to close with a quote Heiner Herzogs, who has really dedicated his entire life's work and love to the museum and folk culture, and whom I wish from my heart that he can realize his project of revitalization and rejuvenation of this 90-year-old and, unfortunately, currently in deep sleep, folklore museum in Graz. Heiner Herzog says: Grown identity is a prerequisite for living tolerance. Thank you.

Mr Cap said that the introduction of the study contributions had been done without my information. – That's wrong. It was a common discussion process. I think that was a rounded package that will be good for the universities in the sense of all of us. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP as well as on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Someone has made the point that one has to trust to be against it. Ladies and gentlemen! Someone who is in a responsible position in politics must trust to do the necessary thing at the right time. [[Abg. Shashing: But not so!]] That was the right time for the necessary! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] With regard to the question raised by Mrs Glawischnig here concerning the Museum Quarter: The museum quarter will be completed as soon as possible. There is no negative development, there is no sluggish planning. We have used a very good managing director. Construction will be completed in the year 2000 – at the end of 2000. The settlement of the museums will take place. These will be opened in September 2001. The overall opening will take place in September 2002. There is a determination that a lively cultural scene with also changing offers will take place in the museum quarter in a small area in the old Fischer-von-Erlach-Trakt. You have said here how terrible it is because of the company "Public Netbase", which works with state-of-the-art media, with the state-of-the-art computers that really do modern media art: This kind of art and this kind of work is independent of place and time! Especially with the modern media I am not at all bound in any place! These small cultural operators have had a precarium. They were clear from the outset: If the fisherman-by-laugh-trap is restored, they have to look for something else. I mean, you also have to be able to rely on contractual agreements. This "Quartier 21" is now being examined by a specialist working group. It is said where we will find room for these cultural initiatives, and there will be rental contracts. – I think this is a completely normal process that is not directed against anyone. What is still a special concern to me is the whole question of restitutions. Ladies and gentlemen! We should really look at what Austria has already done, especially in the entire field of art and culture. After 1945, the Republic of Austria adopted a total of seven provisions for the replacement of art and cultural goods, as well as two art and cultural adjustment laws, and made the so-called "Mauerbach auction", the auction of the works of art confiscated by the National Socialists in favour of the victims of the Holocaust. We then also passed a new Return Act, for the following reasons: It has been found that it is not morally acceptable that for art items that have been robbed during the war have been resettled after the war and have been retained under the Export Prohibition Act, this Export Prohibition Act is still valid. It was no longer intended to apply to these items of art, which have been restored. That is why the last Restitution Act or Art Restitution Act has been adopted. The Art Return Advisory Board works very intensively. The processing of the entire provenance is a very great work, and this is done with great commitment. With regard to the one question you have raised here, I would really like to clarify everything that is serious: This art recovery law has nothing to do with testamentary orders. In the case of Bloch-Bauer there is an will from 1923. You can't say the will is right or wrong. It is not a matter of this art recovery law and of the art recovery council to find a will from 1923. As a Federal Minister, I have the task of acting on a legal basis. I cannot return, on my own accord, discretion, for any moral reason, millions of values that belong to the citizens and which are in the upper Belvedere, if I have no legal basis for them. Deciding on this will is not the task of the Art Return Council. It's just this Causa Bloch farmer doesn't fall under this law. You have to take note of that, please. As far as the "house of history" and the "house of tolerance" are concerned, the concepts are further forgiven. The experts are working on a common concept. On the issue of exclusion, I would like to point out the following: We will be the next steps to separate the still outstanding Federal Museums. This will be completed by 2003, whereby we believe that it is reasonable to cooperate on the whole question of logistics, billing, staffing, so that small houses do not have these costs again. That is why we will introduce a cooperation model between the Theatermuseum and the Volkskundemuseum. Further renovation of the museums will progress according to the budgetary possibilities. I believe that in a time when you have to take off a large debt mountain in which you have to clean up a budget, you simply have to set priorities. We have focused on Albertina, because it is simply not responsible that the world's largest graphic collection will remain closed longer. The study building will cost 139 million schilling, the storage will cost 91 million schilling. The study building will be opened in 2001, the storage will also be opened, and the renovated palace will be opened in 2002. Then we will try to reorganise the Museum of Art. The fact that these renovations and this changeover to a scientific institution are very successful is shown by the Technical Museum, which we converted with 480 million schilling and set up with 250 million schilling – this is a beautiful contribution – and which now has a super manager, namely the director, Dr. Zuna-Kratky, who manages this house perfectly. As a result, I am particularly pleased that she has been ranked first in a list, with a rating of the 20 best managers in Austria. Imagine: the director of a non-profit organization, unlike many who work in the economy! I think this is a nice sign that we are doing future-oriented museum work!

ParlaMint-AT_2000-09-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00037_d7e23467	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! The proposal of a Federal Home Treaty Act is based on the fact that effective measures against the abuse of seniors are necessary, which occurs only in a few homes, but where it takes place is unbearable. You will all know one case or another from your next environment and you will realize for yourself that there is an urgent need for efficient action. We have therefore discussed the question of what is actually efficient and what can be done. In this context, experts have drawn up the proposal for a German Home Treaty Act, which, as the only possible measure, standardises a contract with regulations, which is possible and which is not possible, and in which it is also possible to define what is possible and what is not possible on the basis of human rights in homes. Therefore, this contract, for contract civil law is a federal matter, should also be adopted in accordance with a federal law. Now there is a counter-proposal by Mr Bartenstein and also by Mr Fekter that we should not do this, but rather an Article 15a Treaty, or that the countries should do so in any way, with the possibility that this will not go from today to tomorrow, but that it will probably only work after a longer negotiation process, and that the question arises whether this can really be dealt with equally in all the federal states. I think there is no need at all to leave that to the federal states if we can define a uniform minimum standard here in the interests of the elderly. [[President Dr. Fischer is taking over the presidency again.]] For example, "NEWS" is what is happening in a low Austrian home, where a patient is included in a netbed, although she is not at all disturbed, is located in an open window and had to be removed from the open window from the family. The Styrian authorities report that there are homes in which the residents of the home are not allowed to ride with the lift, at the risk of a punishment, and in a home in Upper Austria, for example, it is common for room doors to be tightened from the outside by putting a wooden bar under the door locks to prevent opening. If you know this, then it is clear that it is necessary to do something about it as quickly as possible and that it is unbearable to say that there is someone in the federal states to make improvements. You will know such cases, and I believe that the only really efficient form is a federal law. I should therefore like to ask you to agree to this in the further debate. Thank you.

I therefore believe that it is important that extramural facilities such as mobile assistance services and care at home should also be particularly forced, because I do not believe that society will be able to cope with this burden by stationary facilities alone. We must therefore develop parallel structures, we must design a clear picture of the so-called extramural area and the intramural area, the mobile area and the stationary area. ParlaMint-AT_2000-09-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00037_d7e23617	Mr President. High house! Homeowners are not rightless. The people who live in homes do not abandon their citizens' rights at the entrance. However, we of the ÖVP also consider it necessary to provide a better legal framework for home residents, home owners and nurses in two areas. First – under quotations – the "consumer protection" for home residents: I now mean rules on remuneration and performance and transparency on them, termination rights, termination possibilities, minimum contractual content, form requirements for the contracts, but also, which is very important, representation rights for persons who can no longer articulate themselves. This does not necessarily have to be regulated in a federal home agreement law, the competence situation is more contrary to that, but it would be a solution if we could find consumer protection regulations in this area. The second area we urgently need to regulate concerns the fundamental and freedom rights of these inhabitants. In particular, rules will become necessary if measures are to be taken to protect a homer, which means an intervention in personal rights of freedom. Analogous to the accommodation law, we need to develop criteria and procedures that clarify that such measures may only be imposed due to medical, caretaker necessity, therapeutic indication or if they are necessary to protect against self-hazardousness. At the same time, an unbureaucratic control mechanism will be necessary to ensure a rule of law. A Federal Home Treaty Law on Freedom Rights is imaginable, because one should not be able to determine these rights in a contractual way. This protection is to be guaranteed by law, and in this sense it is already being worked together with the countries in the judicial area.
